Chapter 1: What Is Machine Learning?
When you think about it, all machine learning really entails is the ability to teach an algorithm or computer program the best way to improve progressively on a given task. When it comes to research, all machine learning is best viewed at the level of mathematical and theoretical models when concerning the process. Nevertheless, it is more practical to think of it as a way to construct many applications that are able to utilize the improvement. There is more than one way that we can make this idea a reality, but mainly they are recognized in three categories. These categories are reinforcement learning, unsupervised learning, and supervised learning.
As we continue to saturate ourselves with machine learning and artificial intelligence, we also continue to become more and more zealous about technology. Because of this, we will need to stay abreast of the understanding required to identify the various machine learning types that we may come in contact with. For the beginner, this may mean they would have to understand the various machine learning types, as well as exhibiting it all in different applications used. As far as the application creators are concerned, they know that it is important that one should know the machine learning types for all the tasks that you may run into. That way you will be able to create a learning environment that is properly-suited for it and you can understand how it worked.
Supervised Machine Learning
The most well-known machine learning is supervised machine learning. It is also the easiest paradigm to be understood and simple to be implemented. It is so simple that it is usually compared to learning flashcards.
When talking about supervised machine learning, it is oftentimes described as being task-oriented. This is because it becomes very focused on one task, as it continuously feeds the algorithm with examples until the desired task is performed. This will be the exact type of learning you will probably come in contact with as you use these applications:
  • Advertisement Popularity :
Advertisements that could have good performance are often selected as a task for supervised learning. A lot of the advertisements that are found online while browsing has been put there with the help of an algorithm which believed that it was popular. It is also, sometimes, due to an algorithm that is associated with a query search where finds could be a good match for the ad placement.
  • Classification of Spam :
If you have ever sent or received an email, then you should be familiar with the spam folder. Your spam folder is a system of supervised learning. This system was fed with many email examples so that it would filter email that seems malicious so that no harassment occurs to the user. These can also learn a user’s preferences as the user adds new email tabs.
  • Face Recognition :
If you have ever used social media, then chances are your face has been a part of supervised learning that uses face recognition. So, the next time that you look at a group photo you are a part of and see a tag suggestion, then you will know that a supervised learning algorithm made the suggestion for you.
Unsupervised Learning
With unsupervised learning, it is just the opposite of supervised. It mainly works solo as the algorithm gets fed of data and is then given tools that will help it to understand the data’s properties. It can then learn how to organize, group, and cluster the given data in such a way that humans would be able to distinguish the data after being organized.
The interesting thing about unsupervised learning is that there is a ton of unlabeled data circling the world. Having an algorithm that can gather all of this unlabeled data and turn it into sensible data has great potential to become profitable if continuously utilized in each industry. This can easily lead to an increased amount of productivity throughout.
A good example is if we had all of the world’s published papers and put it all into a database that was fitted with unsupervised learning. It then put the data into groups that could be easily distinguishable by a human and made you aware of the domain’s progress. At this point, you can start your own research, submitting work into the algorithm. As you take notes and complete your write up, you begin to receive suggestions from the algorithm concerning your work, works that suggest help with domain progression, and works that you could possibly cite. Having this type of tool can easily boost your productivity.
This is why we can also say that all unsupervised learning, which is data and property-based, is also considered to be data-driven. This is why the outcomes from an unsupervised task are controlled by both the data’s format and the data itself.
A few of the places where unsupervised learning could appear include:
  • Different Recommender Systems :
Both Netflix and YouTube are excellent examples of a recommender system. That is why they are normally classified as part of the unsupervised learning algorithm. Having information and data about videos can teach an algorithm a lot. Then you throw in a person’s watch history that says the type of videos they have seen and match it with similar videos that have not been seen can allow a recommender system to make suggestions based on the relationship that was created with the data.
  • Personal Buying Habits :
There is a high probability that a database exists which contains your buying habits. It is also probably true that that information has been bought and sold so many times since first being collected. Having these buying habits allow an unsupervised algorithm to group you into different segments. This gives companies the ability to market to you based on the data collected.
  • User Logs Grouped :
Although these face the user less, they still have relevance, as the unsupervised algorithm can easily put issues and user logs into groups. This will allow companies to view the themes of the types of issues their customers are facing and fix them in a timely manner either through product improvements or creating a helpful list of FAQs that can answer simple questions. No matter how you look at it, it is actively done. The same as it is if you have ever reported something wrong through a website. The issue you submitted is normally fed to their unsupervised algorithm, so it can gather it with other similar submissions that need to be reviewed.
Reinforcement Machine Learning
The reinforcement machine learning algorithm is nowhere near the same as the supervised and unsupervised algorithms. As compared to the unsupervised and supervised (which are based on labels being present), the reinforcement algorithm can seem a lot more complex, although people have described it simply as being a label sequence which is dependent on time. No matter what it means though, just make sure that you are not left being confused.
A better way that it could be looked at is if it learns from any mistakes it makes. This would hold true if you were to deploy it into any type of environment and begin to make mistakes as it started to work. We could then be able to control the algorithm by sending it different signals to use. These signals would also differentiate good and bad behavior so we would be able to reinforce it to make good behavior instead of bad behavior. Then, as time passes, our reinforcement algorithm will be able to make a significantly less amount of mistakes than it did before.
With reinforcement machine learning being driven by behavior, it has become heavily influenced by the psychology and neuroscience fields. An example of this is Pavlov's dog. If you are familiar with it, then you already know about an agent being reinforced, although it was biological.
But to have a true understanding of reinforcement machine learning, there needs to be a breakdown of an example. So for this, let’s pretend to teach them how to play a video game.
As with all other problems, we will need to have an environment, agent, and ability to connect via feedback. We would then give actions to the agent so they can connect to the environment and change it. We then connect the environment to the agent by giving two signals for it to follow. These signals will be behavior signals.
During the video game, the agent will be our algorithm and the game itself will be our environment. Our agent then has several actions to complete the buttons of the game. We then make each game frame as an updated state and the reward signal will be the score of the game as it changes. Now, as long as all the pieces are connected together, we would have a scenario surrounding a game that is based on reinforcement learning.
Where do we see real-world reinforcement learning taking place at?
  • Video Games :
A video game is the best place where a reinforcement learning algorithm can be found. We know this because we can observe how Google uses their algorithm, AlphaGo and AlphaZero, to play a game. Another good example is our own video game. As of today, there is currently no type of game that has an agent that uses reinforcement learning. But, I would imagine this changing if developers make it be an option.
  • Simulating Industry :
When it comes to the robotic applications, it could be useful to have the tasks completed by the machines rather than coding the processes into them. This would be a safer and cheaper way that could even be less of a failure. Once this gets started, we can take it further where we give it tasks that will save us money for the long haul. What’s more, is that we can even complete simulation trials so our machine will not break.
  •    
  • Management of Resources :
When it comes to managing resources, we can easily look at reinforcement learning to handle diverse environments. It can also handle the balancing of certain amounts of requirements. A good example is the data centers located at Google. They are constantly using reinforcement learning to keep power requirements balanced efficiently. This instantly cuts costs for the long term. So, how does this directly affect all of us? Simple, the costs for data storage becomes cheaper and a minimal impact on our environment is created.
What it All Means
Now that we have learned the fundamentals for the three machine learning categories, we must realize that a lot of times, the three categories will merge or one learning method may look like another learning method. This occurs because there are many types of tasks that can make it hard to phrase them accurately.
The recommender system is a great example of this. As we saw, we discussed it mainly as an unsupervised learner. It also has the ability to become supervised. This would be so if it was given the watch histories of many users. It would be able to predict several films that a person may be interested in or make a recommendation. The purpose of this is to acknowledge that it is all learning no matter how you view it. The difference is just the way the problem gets phrased. This is why some problems get phrased in more one ways than another.
This also brings up a second thought. We can easily combine these learning types, but then give them the ability to be integrated on a larger scale within an algorithm.
  • For the agent playing the video game? We could allow it to use supervised learning to label all recognized enemy.
  • Classifying sentences in a system? We could allow it to understand the meaning of sentences that would be generated by an unsupervised learning algorithm.
  • Want to have the ability to gather a group of people into separate groups and segments via a social network? Adding a process of reinforcement will define a person’s representation which will allow them to be clustered in an accurate way.
Remember, there is nothing wrong with having a bit of knowledge about the process of machine learning. This holds true even if we may never be entwined in the technology where we are able to create it ourselves. With an ever-changing world of machine learning, it continues to progress at such a fast rate that we already see several instances of it in real life. Even if we are able to understand the fundamentals, we may never fully be able to navigate a world of technology that could make humans obsolete.
Machine Learning Solving Problems
There is no doubt that both Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning have become more prominent throughout the years. With companies like Amazon, Google, and Microsoft Azure creating Cloud platforms based on Machine Learning, the future looks even brighter. But, the more surprising aspect of it all is that we have not even noticed ourselves experiencing it. The most prominent cases are used by Facebook when an image is tagged and email providers who utilize the machine learning to detect “SPAM”. Facebook has now taken it a step farther where uploaded photos are tagged automatically by their image recognition and Gmail now recognizes certain patterns that allow it to recognize spam. Now, let’s review a few problems that businesses experience which machine learning could rectify.
Spam Detection
Detecting spam was the first problem to be solved by Machine Learning. It was only four years ago that we saw email providers recognizing spam by using pre-existing techniques that were rule-based. But today, we now see the spam filters using machine learning to create the rules that they use. This is possible because the spam filters now use neural networks. Because of the use of neural networks, Google now has a spam rate of only 0.1 percent. Having a neural network as part of a spam filter allows it to learn and then apply what it learns to recognize any phishing or junk. This is accomplished by rules that are analyzed among many computers. Besides detecting spam, social networks have also begun to use Machine learning to help identify abuse and filter it out.
Inefficient Data Entry
Having a lot of duplication and inaccuracies can cause a lot of problems for businesses that wish to implement automation. This is why an algorithm for Machine learning and predictive modeling would easily make their situation improved. Machine learning uses all of the new data to make the process improved as it makes calculations. This allows the machines to perform
time-intensive documentation from what it learned. This frees up a lot more time for the employees to concentrate on more important tasks.
Making Product Recommendations
Having an unsupervised learning algorithm will enable a business to create a recommendation system solely based on products. When the Machine Learning is given a customer’s history of purchases and product inventory, all the Machine Learning needs to do is identify products that may interest the customer thus making a purchase more likely. To do this, the algorithm recognizes product patterns that are hidden and uses this to group similar products. Using this type of model to decide could easily create a program’s recommendation so that a customer would feel more motivated to buy a product. This is what Amazon uses throughout. With Facebook, they use this to make user recommendations.
Customer Segmentation and Lifetime Value Prediction
There are three challenges marketer’s face today. These are customer lifetime value prediction, churn prediction, and customer segmentation. Many businesses have a lot of data related to marketing which stems from many different sources. These sources include lead data, visitors to their website, and email campaigns. As Machine Learning and data mining are used, they can create accurate predictions when making individual marketing offers that can also achieve many incentives. When marketers use Machine Learning, all of the guesswork can be eliminated from the marketing.
Medical/Healthcare
There are many cases that have shown Machine Learning capable of making diagnoses that are near perfect, identify patients that are high-risk, predict readmissions, and recommend the best medicines to prescribe. The created predictions surround a dataset comprised of medical records and patient symptoms. The implementation of Machine Learning is taking off like wildfire in the medical field regardless of the possible hurdles. These hurdles can be easily handled by the practitioners if they understand the medical, technical and legal hurdles involved.
Banking and Financial analysis
The banking and financial market has a huge amount of data that is both historical and quantitative in nature. This makes it perfect to use machine learning to fix any problems seen within the realm of banking and finance. Currently, there are quite a bit of Machine Learning algorithms in use for financial analysis. A few of these are loan underwriting, fraud detection, algorithmic trading, and portfolio management. For loan underwriting, machine learning will continue to enable data assessments used for analysis and detection of possible nuances and anomalies. This will allow the rules and models to be improved and precise. This precision will also eliminate a lot of positions related to underwriting.
Image recognition (Computer Vision)
For image recognition, it has the ability to produce and process symbolic and numerical information from all high-dimensional and image data. For it to be successful, it needs to involve pattern recognition, data mining, machine learning, and database knowledge discovery. Many uses in business can be found in the automobile sector and healthcare.
Predictive Maintenance
In manufacturing, they can easily combine machine learning and artificial intelligence to uncover factory data patterns that could have high meaning because the maintenance of preventative and corrective practices can become inefficient and expensive. But, when predictive maintenance is used, the risk is minimized for any failures that could occur and the activities for preventative maintenance are reduced.
The machine learning architecture for predictive maintenance can also be created using an analysis environment, historical data, a feedback loop for operations, and a visualization tool for workflow. The machine learning platform for Microsoft AZURE takes it one step further by providing simulated aircraft engine examples during events to show how they use their modeling process for predictive maintenance. This process allegedly has a progressing degradation pattern. The pattern can be seen throughout the sensor management’s assets. To be able to predict failures in the future, the machine learning algorithm needs to learn the relationship between sensor value changes and sensor values.
Algorithms Used in Machine Learning
A machine learning algorithm is a specifically designed program that is capable of receiving data, and thus, learning and improving from it through experience and on its own. A few learning tasks involved are how a hidden structure is found within an unlabeled dataset, figuring out the function that connects the output to an input and finding the location of a class label.
Linear Regression
Machine learning is comprised of many variables for input and is used to determine the variable for output. This shows that there is an existing relationship between the output variable and the input variable. The machine learning goal is to measure the current relationship.
In regards to Linear Regression, the input and output variables make up an equation expressed as y = a + bx. This means that the goal is to discover both of the value of the coefficients.
Advantages
Linear regression is simple to use. The method is also very intuitive to understand. If the data is not an exact fit, it can still be used to find the relationship between the variables.
Shortcomings
Linear Regression Only Works With Linear Relationships
Linear regression uses variables that have independent and dependent relationships. This means it is assumed that the relationship has a straight line. This is not correct all of the time. A good example of this is that there is a curve in an age and income relationship. This means that an income rises in the early years and then eventually declines after retirement begins. This can be problematic after viewing the relationship’s graphical representation.
The Dependent Variable’s Mean is Only Looked at by Linear Regression
The independent and dependent variables’ relationship is viewed by linear regression through its mean. An example is when you view the relationship between a mother’s age and their baby’s weight, then the linear regression will view the different ages of mothers that have babies with average weight.
Outliers Make Linear Regression Sensitive
An outlier can be a surprising piece of data. They can also be multivariate or univariate. For example, when looking at income and age, a univariate outlier is when a 100-year-old person had a yearly income of $10 million. A multivariate outlier would be a person under 20 who makes $100,000. As you can see, income and age are not extreme, but the difference is that not many young people make that much in a year.
Independent Data
It is assumed that it is independent data. This means that one subject is independent of another. Most of the time, this is not sensible. We can see this in the same sense of time and space being clustered.
A space clustering example is when there is a similarity among test scores by students. This happens because the students come from different grades, classes, districts, and schools. When students are in the same class, they are normally the same. The teachers and neighborhoods are the same, so they cannot be considered independent.
A time clustering exists when a subject is measured many times. An example of this is when you study weight and diet. That person may be measured many times. This does not allow for independent data because the weight of a person is related to each occasion. A way this can be dealt with is by using multilevel models.
CART
A CART (Classification and Regression Trees) is an implemented Decision Tree. The root node and internal node are the non-terminal nodes of a CART. The leaf nodes are the terminal nodes. A single input terminal is represented by a non-terminal node and splitting point with leaf nodes are represented by the output. The predictions in the model are made in the following way: you walk up the tree splits and arrive at the leaf node and take out the value found in the leaf node.
Advantages
☐         Nonparametric (no assumptions of probability)
☐         Variable selection is performed automatically
☐         Continuous and discrete variables are used in any combination
☐         The feature is nice: variables that are categorical are binned automatically by category.
☐         The variable interactions are discovered
☐         Used to search rules
☐         Models are hybrid
☐         Missing values are automatically handled
☐         Surrogate splits are used
☐         Monotonic transformations have predictable variables
☐         Good for visualizing and exploring data
Shortcomings
☐         The model is not continuous, but a step function.
☐        The possible values have to equal the number of nodes which is improved by MARS.
☐        A good lift may only occur if the tree is large. This may make the interpretation hard. Each split makes the data thinner.
☐        The model structure is unstable. Different trees could develop out of fluctuations in data and variables.
☐         The linear structure is poorly modeled by CART.
Logistic Regression
All predictions made by linear regression have values that are continuous. Discrete values are represented by logistic regression after a transformation function is applied.
Binary classification best fit logistic regression because the y data sets are 0 or 1 when the default class is denoted by 1. An example of this is when an event is predicted to occur or not to occur, no more than two possibilities are available: that it not happen (0) or does happen (1). So, as we make a prediction concerning a sick patient, the data set we use would reflect a 1.
The name logistic regression came about from the logistic function which is a transformation function and is expressed as h(x) = 1/ (1 + ex) and is formed into an S curve.
The output in logistic regression is viewed as the default class’s probabilities (which are different from linear regression as it is produced directly). The probability is also that the range of the output is 0-1. So, when we are attempting to make a prediction of sick patients, we will already have them entered with a 1. This means that if a patient is given a score of 0.99, then the algorithm will tell us that the patient has a high percentage to get sick.
When the log transforms the x value this generates the y value output by way of the function h(x)= 1/ (1 + e^ -x) . You then apply a threshold that causes the probability to be forced together to the binary classification.
In determining a malignant tumor, we use the y variable as the default (malignant). For the x, it could be the size of the tumor. The x is then transformed by the logistic function throughout the data set instances that range from 0-1. If a 0.5 probability is crossed, then it is considered to be a malignant tumor.
The equation representing logistic regression is P(x) = e ^ (b0 +b1x) / (1 + e(b0 + b1x))  and is easily transformed to ln(p(x) / 1-p(x)) = b0 + b1x . Logistic regression’s goal is to have the training data find the value of the coefficient of b0 and b1 so the error will be minimal among the actual and predicted outcome. The MLE technique will then estimate both coefficients.
For a tumor to be determined as malignant, the y=1 must be the default variable. For the variable x, this is the tumor’s size or measurement. An example of this is the transformation of the x value by the logistic function that makes different data set instances if it falls in the 0-1 range. If the probability crosses the threshold of 0.5 (shown by the horizontal line), the tumor is classified as malignant.
Advantages
☐         Considered a very simple algorithm for machine learning and efficient.
☐         Has low variance.
☐         Feature extraction can be used.
☐         Easily updates logistic models using new stochastic gradient descent data.
☐         Easily updates logistic models.
Shortcomings
Can Fail at Identifying Independent Variables
Many predictions are made using independent variables, but if wrong variables are entered, the predictive value is next to none. An example is if a college admission is based on test scores and not letters of recommendation, then the researchers forget to input the data concerning the letter of recommendations, then the overall accuracy or usefulness of the model will be worthless. This classifies Logistic regression as being a tool that could be useless. Of course, this would be different if the independent variables have been identified by the researchers.
Outcome Variables Are Limited
Logistic regression is great for categorical predictions for specific outcomes like college rejections or admissions. It also works well with multinomial prediction outcomes like the college waitlist. In spite of this, continuous outcomes cannot be predicted by logistic regression. An example is when it could not predict the fever of an influenza patient and if it would become high, due to the temperature being a continuous measurement. Researchers could, however, categorize the temperature measurement as either “low” or “high,” but this could make the data unprecise. This disadvantage is very significant when continuous scales are being tested.
Requires Independent Observations
It is required that each data point of logistic regression is independent of one another. If any observations become related, this will make the model overweigh the observations’ significance. This disadvantage is a major one due to so much research poured into the techniques that require the same subjects to be observed multiple times. An example is drug trials that compare individuals based on their pair design as one takes a placebo and one takes the drug. This is why studies cannot rely on the logistic regression technique.
Model Becomes Over Fitted
Logistic regression uses independent variables to make predictions, but can often cause logit models to be vulnerable and cause overconfidence. This means they look to be predictive but really are not due to bias. As we saw in the college example, a few applicants might generate a model that could predict many students having high SAT and GPAs which would be admitted instantly. However, a small amount of these applicants might be rejected. Therefore, using logistic regression would be considered “overfit” because the prediction’s accuracy is overstated.
Naïve Bayes
Probability of an event is calculated with Bayes Theorem as long as one event has occurred already. For a hypothesis to be true, we calculate its probability based on the knowledge and use the Theorem in the following way:
P(h|d)= (P(d|h) P(h)) / P(d)
whereas:
  • The P(h|d) = Posterior probability. The hypothesis is considered true based on data d as P(h|d)= P(d1| h) P(d2| h)….P(dn| h) P(d)
  • If P(d|h) = Likelihood. This data d probability based on a true hypothesis.
  • P(h) = Probability of prior class. Hypothesis probability being true regardless of data.
  • P(d) = Probability of prior Predictor. The data probability regardless of the hypothesis.
The reason why “naïve” is used is that the algorithm assumes the variables to be independent of one another, thus making it naïve in the real-world.
Advantages
☐        Results become better for the problems with little data amounts used for all the training. This is caused because of the low propensity.
☐         Training is quick and involves the priors and likelihoods in the computation.
☐        Quick prediction occurs for all new data points. Each posterior class is calculated. The rule gets applied, then labeling class occurs.
☐         Quick prediction for data point
☐        Ram memory has a modest footprint due to the no requirement of the data set to be held at once in the RAM.
☐        Modest usage of CPU. No iterative updates to parameter gradients or computing needed because of analytic formulae being employed.
☐        Is linearly scaled with all data point numbers and features which makes it easy for new data to be updated.
☐        Its light CPU usage, a small amount of memory, linear scaling, and fast execution provides many solutions that are viable for big problems that may be too intensive.
☐        Missing value features are easily handled which is conducted with predicting and re-training.
Shortcomings
☐         Feature interactions cannot be incorporated
☐        Likelihoods could be hard to calculate for regression problems. Assigning discrete classes and data binning is considered sub-optimal due to information being thrown away. Every feature assumed normal and workable is distributed, but performance may be impacted if not distributed normally. On the split side, the likelihood can be estimated as long as there is enough training data.
☐        Skewed data makes performance sensitive. This means the training data is not given good class distributions for the representation of the population. For this reason, all prior estimates are not correct.
Apriori
With the Apriori algorithm, we use it for mining in a transactional database to look for item sets before generating the association rules. Apriori is used frequently among the market basket analysis. This is where product combinations are data checked for the co-occurring. Generally, the rule for the association is written: X -> Y where if X is purchased, then Y will also be purchased.
An example of this is when sugar and milk are purchased. There could be a high probability that a person may also buy coffee. This too could be representative of a basic association rule that reads sugar, milk -> coffee. An association rule gets generated after the confidence and support threshold has been crossed.
Having a support measure allows the candidate’s item sets to be pruned as they are considered while generation occurs in the item set. It is a guided support measure of the Apriori principle. According to the Apriori principle, if there is a frequent itemset, then the other subsets need to be frequent.
Advantages
  • Property for item set must be large
  • Parallelized easily
  • Implements easily
Shortcomings
  • Suffers many trade-offs and inefficiencies that affect different algorithms.
  • Many subsets are generated by candidate generation.
  • Many candidate sets get loaded to the algorithm at one time.
  • The database is scanned more times than necessary and makes the performance reduced. This allows the algorithm to make an assumption of the database.
  • Maximal subsets are found after proper subset exploration.
  • Very high and complex algorithm for space and time. This makes it exponential in not knowing where the horizontal width is and if the database includes it.
Other algorithms make many attempts to make maximal frequent item identification by not enumerating any of the subsets. This creates a search space “jump” instead of a bottom-up approach.
KNN
The KNN algorithm is used as a training set for the data set instead of having the data set split into the test set and training set.
If the KNN requires a data instance outcome, the algorithm completes the data set, as it tries to find the instance nearest to the k number or k-nearest that is the closest in similarity. This then creates the output for the outcomes mode or mean for each classification.
The k value is specified by the user. The instances of similarity are calculated with measures of Hamming and Euclidean distance.
Advantages
  • Is very simple and intuitive: The KNN easily understands and implements easily too. The KNN algorithm goes through a dataset in order to find the nearest neighbors to classify it.
  • No assumptions: There are assumptions in the KNN algorithm, so there will be assumptions as it is implemented. This is what makes it great being a non-parametric algorithm.
  • No Training Required for the KNN: This is because no model is built, it simply uses the historical data. Any new data is then tagged along with a neighbor.
  • It evolves constantly because it is instance-based and having an approach that is memory-based. Its classifier adapts immediately while training data is collected. It also allows for a quick response of the algorithm as changes are made to the output.
  • KNN implements easily to handle multi-class problems: A lot of the classifiers are implemented easily to handle the binary problems and adjusts perfectly to the multi-class.
  • Both regression and classification can be used which makes it a great advantage for KNN.
  • KNN Requires a single hyperparameter: All other parameters align to the first parameter.
  • The multitude of distance criteria available: The KNN algorithm allows flexibility, so the user can choose the distance as the model is built.
Shortcomings
  • The algorithm can be slow. This is due to the amount of algorithm efficiency and speed.
  • Unable to be dimensional: Although KNN works great with many variables, it still seems to struggle with predicting new data point output.
  • Homogenous features needed for KNN: The same scale is necessary for building a KNN that has common distance. This is because the absolute distances weight the same in the features. This means the distance in feature 1 and 2 must be the same.
  • The number of neighbors is optimal: This is considered a big issue as new data entry is being classified.
  • Problems are caused by imbalanced data: Imbalanced data is not a good idea for KNN as its performance is subpar. If two classes are considered A or B, and A has the majority of training data, then A will be preferred more and B could be classified wrong.
  • Sensitive Outlier: The algorithm is sensitive to the outliers because of the distance of the neighbors.
  • Value Treatment is Missing: No capability to deal with the problem of missing data.
K-means
Being an iterative algorithm allows it to form clusters from groups of data. K clusters and centroids are calculated as a data point is assigned to each cluster that has a short distance from a data point.
This is how it works:
First, the value of k is chosen. So, let’s say 3=k. We then assign the data points to one of the 3 clusters randomly. You then compute the cluster centroids of the clusters. After that, each point is reassigned to a close cluster. Next, the centroids are calculated for any clusters that are new. Lastly, steps 2-3 are repeated until the switching of points has stopped between the clusters. Once switching has stopped during 2 steps, the K-means algorithm gets existed.
Advantages
  • Implementation is easy.
  • Data sets are large scale.
  • Convergence is guaranteed.
  • The centroids position can be warm-started.
  • New examples are adapted easily.
  • Different cluster sizes and shapes, like elliptical clusters, are generalized.
Shortcomings
  • K can be manually chosen. Finding the optimal K is completed by using the plot listed as “Loss vs. Clusters”.
  • Initial values being depended on
  • The dependence for low k can be mitigated after k-means is run a few times with a variety of values and the best result is picked. As the k is increased, advanced versions are needed so that better values can be picked from the centroids (referred to as seeding).
  • Different densities and sizes of data can be clustered.
  • Clustering data is difficult for k-means when the cluster is of different density and size. For the data to be clustered, the k-means needs to be generalized.
  • Outliers are clustered.
  • Outliers can drag centroids as well as receive a cluster if they feel like they are being ignored. To avoid this, outliers can be clipped or removed prior to being clustered.
  • The number of dimensions increases with scaling.
  • With an increase of dimensions, the similarity measure is converged and turned into a constant that is between examples. Dimensionality is reduced by way of a feature data, spectral clustering, or a PCA. Either of these can allow the clustering algorithm to be modified.
Principal Component Analysis
The PCA is designed to make it easy to visualize and explore data by having the variables reduced. This is completed after the maximum variance is captured in the data, and then added to the coordinate system by way of the principal components.
The components are a linear combination of variables that are original and are considered orthogonal of each other. When orthogonality occurs, this indicates a zero correlation between the components.
The maximum variability’s direction is captured during the initial principal component. For the next principal component, the remaining variance is captured within the data and has uncorrelated variables towards the initial component. All other successive PC’s will then become uncorrelated to the prior PC as the variance that remains is captured.
Advantages
  • Features of Correlation are Removed: Having a lot of dataset features is common in the real world. The thing that matters though is the amount your algorithm can run. Unfortunately, this algorithm is unable to run this many features, so they need to be reduced in order to perform better.
  • As you do this, the correlation of the feature needs to be found out.
  • It can seem impossible to find the correlation between the features. It can also be very time consuming and frustrating. Having PCA will do this efficiently for you.
  • After your dataset has been implemented with the PCA, all PC’s then become independent. No correlation remains.
  • Performance improves in the algorithm: Since it has countless features, the algorithm’s performance will decrease tremendously. Having a PCA will easily allow your algorithm to increase speed. This is done by ridding the variables that are correlated and is not contributing to the decision-making process. This reduces the algorithm’s training time significantly with a low amount of features.
  • So, if you have high input dimensions, then your best bet to speed things up with your algorithm is to utilize a PCA.
  • Overfitting is Reduced: When a lot of variables are present in the dataset, then overfitting occurs. Having PCA will assist with the overfitting becoming too problematic by making the feature amount reduced.
  • Visualization is improved: Having high dimensions makes it very difficult to understand and visualize the data. The PCA turns any data that has high dimensions into data that has low dimensions. This is how PCA makes it easily visualized.
  • Using 2D Screen Plot will allow you to view the high variance of the PC and which will have the most impact when compared to others.
What is great is that PCA can even be used to reduce a 4-dimensional IRIS dataset. It can easily be reduced to a 2-dimensional so that visualization can be improved.
A situation to consider is one that has 40 features (p=40). There is also p(p-1)/2  of plots that are possible to have the relationship’s variable analyzed. Having the data analyzed for exploratory purposes would be very tedious. This is why having PCA is great to have if you have a problem like this.
Shortcomings
Interpretation becomes difficult for Independent Variables: All of your original features will become PC’s after PCA becomes implemented on a dataset. The original features are made up of the linear combination of the principal components. Original features are more interpretable and readable than PCs.
Standardization of data is required prior to PCA: Your data must be standardized prior to PCA being implemented. If it’s not, then the optimal PC will not be located by PCA.
 
For example, for feature sets that have data measured in Kilograms, Millions, or Light years, then the scale for variance will be significant among training sets. If a feature set has PCA applied, then the feature’s loadings that have high variance will be significant as well. This will cause bias among PCs and high variance features which could result in false readings. This also means that categorical features must be representative of numerical features prior to the application of PCA.
Scale affects PCA, so the features need to be scaled in any data prior to the application of PCA. After you learn standardization, you can apply it to dataset features by standardizing them on unit scales (where 0 = mean and 1 = standard deviation), as this is required on ML algorithms that provide high performance.
 
Loss of Information: PCs are continuously trying to have maximum variance covered in a dataset’s features, but what we must remember to do is take care when selecting the number of PCs or some information may be missed once the list of features is compared to it.
 
AdaBoost Boosting
Adaptive Boosting is also known as AdaBoost. While a bagging ensemble is parallel to each independently-built model, boosting represents an ensemble that is sequential with the models built with the notion of correcting the previous models' misclassification.
Bagging, on the other hand, includes a voting system. This occurs when votes are taken by a classifier in order for an outcome to be achieved and where parallel models make the final determination. With boosting, a system of voting is weighted by every individual classifier to determine an outcome that is obtainable by the majority, however, all sequential models built were assigned a weight greater than the instances of misclassification that models previously saw. For the process of weak learners being constructed, it continues until the weak learner’s amount is constructed or no more improvement is needed during training.
To determine this, we must first start a decision tree with a stump so a decision can be made on it.
To be sure that equal weights have been applied, the data points must show this size in order to be classified correctly as a triangle or circle. For the stump, a horizontal line is generated in the top part used for classifying the points. By this, we see a couple of circles that have been predicted incorrectly. For this, higher weights must be assigned along with another stump for decisions.
Next, we go to a different stump of a decision tree where we make another variable decision.
This time, it is observed that a misclassified circle’s size is larger than the points that remain. This decision tree will also attempt to predict the circles in the correct way.
The result of adding a higher weight shows that the circles were classified correctly by a vertical line. The bad part is that misclassification has occurred among the top circles. These too will receive higher weights as well as a stump.
Now, train another tree stump to make another input variable decision. With this, we see that the circles that were misclassified are now larger than the data points. A vertical line is now placed to show that the triangles and circles have been classified.
Now, the decision stumps should be combined.
The separators are now combined from all 3 models and see the model’s rule classifies the data points in a correct way when compared to the weak learners.
Advantages
☐         Fast
☐         Program is easy and simple.
☐         No need to tune parameters
☐         Flexibility allows it to work with all algorithms.
☐         Weak learner knowledge is not needed.
☐        Effective and provable consistently finds rules of thumb as rough with a shift in the mindsets. The goal is to find all classifiers that are better than just guessing.
☐         Versatile
☐         Discrete, textual, numeric data can be used
☐         Learning has been extended so many problems can be learned and classified
Shortcomings
☐         AdaBoost’s performance is dependent on the weak learner and data.
☐         The theory is consistent but can fail if classifiers are complex and overfitting.
☐         Classifiers are too weak, overfitting, underfitting, low margins
☐         Uniform noise can make AdaBoost very susceptible
Bagging with Random Forests
With bagging, what we need to do first is have multiple models created together with data sets by way of the method, Bootstrap sampling. With this method, random subsamples are created by using a data set to generate training sets. The sizes of these data sets are the same as the training sets, but a lot of times, records do not show up while others repeat a lot. If this happens, then it can only be used as a test set. For this to work, then each training set must be set to N, the data set gets set to N, as the unique records are around (2N/3); the test set must also be N.
Bagging’s second step involves using the algorithm for multiple model creation on a variety of training sets that have been generated differently. This is when the Random Forests come into play. Random forests are a whole different design as compared to decision trees. This is because Random forests allow random selection for a split as to where a decision tree has nodes that split for the best feature where an error would be the least. The reason why randomness occurs is that regardless of having bagging when a feature is chosen by a decision tree, correlation of prediction and similar structures occur. However, if bagging occurs on features with random subsets after splitting, then the subtree predictions will be less correlated. The split point features will then be considered as a Random Forest algorithm parameter.
So, when we conduct bagging with Random Forest, each random record of samples creates the tree, while the random predictor sample creates the splits.
Advantages
  • The Random Forest uses the technique of ensemble learning as part of the bagging algorithm. A lot of trees are created based on data subsets and intertwines all trees to the output. This will reduce the amount of overfitting in any decision tree as well as improving all of the accuracies by reducing any variance.
  • Regression and classification problems can be solved by using Random Forest.
  • Variables that are continuous and categorical work well with Random Forest.
  • Missing values are automatically handled by a Random Forest.
  • Does not require any feature scaling: Feature scaling is not required which includes cases of Random Forest that have normalization and standardization because of using an approach that is rule-based.
  • Efficiently handles parameters that are non-linear: A Random Forest’s performance is not affected by nonlinear parameters, unlike algorithms that are curve-based. So, if independent variables have a high non-linearity, then outperformance may occur with the Random Forest, unlike the algorithms that are curve-based.
  • Missing values are automatically handled by a Random Forest.
  • A Random Forest is strong and healthy and are able to automatically handle them.
  • A Random Forest is a very stable algorithm. So, if new points of data are presented in datasets, the algorithm does not go through much changes overall because one tree may be impacted by the new data, so it may also be difficult to impact them all.
  • Noise is unable to make a significant impact on a Random Forest.
Shortcomings
  • Very complex: Many trees are created with a Random Forest (unlike a decision tree that only makes one tree) and their outputs are combined. In the Python Sklearn library, 100 trees are created by default. In order for this to occur, the algorithm must have the required amount of resources and computational power. On the split side, a decision tree requires little resources for computations making it very simple.
The training period is longer: The training period for a Random Forest takes more time, unlike a decision trees training time because of the number of trees created (unlike that of a decision tree) and the decisions that are made on the majority of the votes.
How Can Machine Learning Help Business
Many resources and algorithms are available for machine learning which can be accessed via many channels that are open source. Services include cloud computing and allow for businesses to utilize the machine learning to help them ensure that their improved services get delivered to all of their customers with no required upfront investment in the infrastructure resources. With the technology close to maturity, many organizations have begun to take the advantages associated in these ways:
Elimination of Manual Tasks
For the majority of the 20th century, using machines was the main focus of all industrial automation because of its ability to have manual tasks that were considered as repetitive and predictable reduced. Not only that, but the industrial automation has become more and more unreliable in having manual operations fully replaced due to the need of certain manual parameters, internal changes to the system, and other external factors that were seen as unpredictable. Machine Learning’s introduction allowed the technology to help fill a void by using models that were predictive and could be applied to real-time data points, having tasks of automation executed accordingly, and having decision support delivered. Within the last few decades, many applications involving machine learning have moved past the automation industry and businesses that are considered software support to the customers and end-users of the organization itself.
Decision Making in Real-Time
Accurate information is relied upon by businesses so that they make the best decisions possible at that moment. Because of being connected so much, it could seem very impossible to get the most accurate information out there if no technology was intelligent enough to provide it from Big Data. Not only does machine learning allow transformations to occur, but organizations data sets are turned into intelligence that is actionable and knowledge. This information is then integrated and turned into processes that can be used every day for any operational activity so to keep up with market and business needs. The results of this are businesses using machine learning will be able to maintain their ability to watch the competition as well as take important measures that will help maintain their real-time edge over the competition.
Operating Expense is Reduced
When you consider any customer support case, a business that has a user-base that is always large will always seem to struggle with the high demands of its customers. This also includes the high customer support demand that is ever-present via web-based chat or phone. To counter this high demand, companies are forced to employ many new support positions that can alieve the demand. This also includes the technology required like the connections and the telephone systems as well as a good strategy that will optimize the supports effectiveness and speed. When machine learning is implemented with technologies like cars or chat-bots, the tasks can be automatically performed to identify problems that the customer may be having so they get guided to the information that fits the problem. This will decrease cost, provide more accuracy, and decrease the amount of wait time to talk to a human being.
Services and Business Models are Improved
As huge enterprises continue to dominate every market, many organizations continue to seek an edge in other markets, so that they do not get completely shut out of their industry. When it comes to mid-size and small businesses, this type of edge stems from the type of business model, services, and innovative types of products offered. An example of this includes both AirBnB and Uber and how they both have utilized technologies associated with machine learning to develop their business models. Having machine learning allowed these companies to become empowered to provide experiences and search results that are accurate. This can also happen to any other companies that might have plans in the future for machine learning.
Network and Security Performance Enhanced
With many anomalies, cyber threats, and intrusions occurring every day, it can be difficult to prevent them if they happen in real-time. In order to maintain an organizations network and keep it secure, any behavior deemed as unwarranted or suspicious must be proactively identified prior to it turning into a full-on attack where service outages occur and data leaks run rampant. Having an algorithm based on machine learning will make it easier to keep a network monitored for real-time behavior purposes and that measures are proactively executed automatically. Also, as the algorithms begin to self-train, an improvement occurs among cyber-security where manual research and adaptability begins. Then the analysis is used to unveil insights into security specifics that every organization needs for its network.
Basic Terminology in Machine Learning
Are you just getting your feet wet with AI? Perhaps, you already have but want to explore more of the world of machine learning so that you can increase your knowledge of every subject available, but not have the time for, until now? Having this glossary of machine learning terms will give you a good introduction into this world we call machine learning. You will learn some of the most technical terms of machine learning that are for both the technical and commercially-interested learner. In no way will it make you exhaustive, but will give you a good edge if read as a prep tool, perhaps prior to an interview for a job or before an important vendor meeting.
Natural Language Processing or NLP
With NLP (Natural Language Processing), it is a notion that is very common among many methods of machine learning that allows a computer to fully perform and understand operations by using natural, human language as it is written or spoken.
Dataset
An essential part of machine learning is the data. To be able to build any system for machine learning, you will need to either obtain the data from a source or find it yourself. Once you find and gather all the data to be used for either testing or building your machine learning, then it is referred to as the dataset. All data scientists basically divide all datasets into three types of groups.
Data for Training
All data for training is what is used for model training. This means that a model for machine learning learns to see the data and learn to determine the features or detect the patterns considered as important for prediction.
Data for Validation
All data used for validation tune the model parameters and compare the different models to decide which one is the best. Not only must the validation data have a different purpose than the training data, but it also must not be used for that purpose. If it is, then it would be considered overfit and would not be able to become production data.
Test Data
Although tedious, a third and final test is always going to occur. Usually referred to as a hold-out, this is used after a final model has been chosen for stimulating the behavior of the model where data is unseen. These would be the data points left over from the building of the model or in the decision-making process for the type of model to use.
CV (Computer Vision)
A CV (Computer vision) is an Artificial Intelligence (AI) field that concerns itself with analysis tools and understanding high-level video data and images. The common computer vision problems are:
Classification of Image
Classification of the image is also referred to as Image classification and is a type of CV that works with teaching recognition to a model so it can recognize items in an image. An example of this is when a model is trained to recognize different items of a public area (as it could easily be with self-driving cars).
Detecting Objects
Detecting objects is another CV that teaches a machine learning model on how to detect instances of objects from categories that have been predetermined. It uses a bounding box after it finds an instance of every object. An example of this is where object detection can be used to build a system to recognize facial features. Then, the model could use the bounding box to draw a square around each detected face. (FYI: the detecting objects system is only for detecting faces in an image and not being able to tell where the location of it is.)
Segmentation of Image
Segmentation of image is a task that trains a machine learner model how a pixel with class is annotated by way of a predetermined set that the pixel most likely is a part of.
Detection of Saliency
Detection of saliency is a specified task that trains a machine learning model on how to show an area that could be the most interesting to a viewer. This information could lead to the placement of ads in a video.
Supervised Learning
A machine learning model comprised of a family is also known as supervised learning and where they are able to self-teach. This also means that the data for supervised ML’s tasks are required to have labels. For example, if you build an ML model to help recognize the text of a certain subject, then the model will need examples that are labeled (information concerning the subject + text). If a new example is given that is unseen, then the target is predicted by the model.
Unsupervised Learning
Unlike supervised learning, this model self-teaches by way of observation. The algorithm is given unlabeled data because the algorithm is not given a value for ground truth. With unsupervised learning, the models are capable of finding the input’s different relationships or structure. The best technique available for unsupervised learning is called “clustering”. With clustering, if the data is given, then several other input clusters are created by the model which makes it able to add new input that was unseen previously and place it in a cluster.
Reinforcement Learning
With reinforcement learning, its approach is quite different from other approaches that were earlier described. The algorithm for reinforcement learning begins as a type of game that aims for a reward. As it “plays”, the algorithm attempts several approaches by way of trial-and-error to see which will generate more reward.
The use cases that are the most common include a computer being taught to play chess or unscramble a Rubik’s Cube. Besides these, there are many more learning opportunities than just to play games. As of late, there have been increases in the number of solutions created by reinforcement learning in regards to Bidding in real-time. This is where bidding is done by the model for an ad spot and the conversion rate of the client is considered the reward.
Neural Networks
A neural network consists of a wide variety of ML models. The idea of them all is to be a mimic of the human brain’s behavior as data is processed. This is similar to how brain neurons connect to networks, an artificial neural also have many layers. Every layer represents neurons, where they are used in the detection of several things. Data is sequentially processed, as the direct connection to the input is considered to be from the first layer. Then, each other layer detects the features that are also based on a previous layer’s output. This will give the model the ability to learn many more patterns that have complex data. If the layers rapidly increase, then the model is considered to be Deep Learning. It is for this reason that determining the exact layer amount can be difficult especially when determining it as deep. Today, the amount is over 20, but 10 years ago, it was only around 3.
Many variations are available for Neural Networks. Here are the most common:
Convolutional style neural networks: Considered to be a Computer Vision breakthrough (as of recent, they have proven to also be used with NLP).
Recurrent style neural networks: These process data using sequential methods like stock prices or texts. Although they are old, they have become increasingly popular in recent years by being very feasible to use and train in a good amount of time.
Neural Networks Fully Connected: These models are the simplest to use for tabular/static data.
Overfitting
When a model builds an assumption, it is always a negative effect and considered bias due to its data amount being insufficient. This is an important problem to know about, as it is common. This can be easily interpreted in everyday life as saying: You visit a bakery, and every time, they never have your favorite pastry. You would get upset at the bakery although many others would be satisfied with the variety. If this was a model for machine learning, then you would say that you are overfitting when compared to the example, created a bias and a poor representation and is not considered as accurate.
When overfitting occurs, this means the data’s random noise is being treated by the model and adjusts to it as a significant signal. This is what causes it to deteriorate on new data (as there is a different noise). For complex models, this is usually the case just like Gradient Boosting and Neural Networks.
Imagine a model that detects an article that mentions a certain sport that is part of the Olympics. If a training set is biased against the Olympics, then the model may adopt different features that only recognize the word “Olympics,” thus failing to detect any articles that are correct but do not have the word.

When these technologies are used, you can easily train computers to complete certain tasks by having a lot of data processed and having the data patterns recognized.
